What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I suppose it’s mostly that my head’s been feeling a bit foggy lately. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I’ve been mixing up simple things—appointment times, dates for exhibits, and even which volunteer I already called. It’s probably nothing too serious, but it’s been bothering me enough that I thought I should come in.\n\nDoctor: Are these memory mix-ups making it hard for you to manage your usual day-to-day tasks on your own?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I mean, I still try to keep up with things, but I have missed a couple of doctor visits, and I got the senior center schedule mixed up pretty badly. My husband’s been helping me more with the calls and the calendar, and even with decisions about repairs—things I used to just handle myself. I don’t know if that counts as “hard to manage,” but it’s more than I’d like to admit.\n\nDoctor: Do these memory problems feel new for you, compared with how you used to function?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does feel new, or at least more noticeable than before. I’ve always had the occasional lapse, of course, but this foggy feeling and mixing up simple things—appointments, exhibit dates, who I already called—it’s not really how I used to be. I hope I’m explaining that alright.\n\nDoctor: Did this foggy feeling come on suddenly, or did it build up gradually?\n\nPatient: I think it built up more gradually… at least that’s how it seems to me. It wasn’t like I woke up one day and everything was different. It’s more that I started noticing my head felt foggy, and then I’d mix up little things—appointment times, exhibit dates, or whether I’d already called a volunteer. Nothing dramatic, I suppose, but enough that I began to worry a bit.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it depressed, exactly… maybe a little discouraged now and then. I try not to dwell on it. Some days I feel lower than I’d like, but I don’t want to make too much of it if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying your usual activities less than you used to?\n\nPatient: I suppose… yes, a bit. I don’t want to sound dramatic, but things I used to really love—my watercolors, fussing over my tomato plants, line dancing, even just watching the cardinals out back—lately they just sort of sit there. I see them, and I know I used to care, but I can’t quite make myself feel interested. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or low most days?\n\nPatient: Most days… I’m not sure. I wouldn’t want to overstate it. I do have days where I feel a bit low or sort of weighed down, but I don’t know that I’d call it sad most days. Maybe just… not quite myself. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Have these memory problems made it harder for you to handle things like your medicines, bills, or driving?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I hate to admit that. I’ve missed a couple doctor visits, and I got the senior center schedule all mixed up—worse than I realized. My husband’s been helping with the calls and the calendar now, and even some repair decisions I used to just take care of myself. I suppose it’s been harder than I’d like to say.\n\nDoctor: Has your husband or anyone else close to you noticed that your memory or thinking has been getting worse?\n\nPatient: Yes… my husband has noticed, I think. He tries to be kind about it, but he’ll point out when I’ve gotten mixed up again. That’s really what brought me in—my head feels sort of foggy, and I keep confusing simple things, like appointment times or exhibit dates, or even which volunteer I already called. I know everyone forgets things at my age, but this feels a bit more than that… if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually cold lately?\n\nPatient: Unusually cold? No, I don’t think so… not that I’ve really noticed. I mean, I get chilly now and then, but nothing that’s stood out to me as different. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: A little, maybe… I don’t like to make too much of it. My husband says I move like I’m wading through mud now. Even just getting up to answer the phone, or sorting through the mail, seems to take me such a long time. I suppose he’s right, though I wish it didn’t sound so bad.\n\nDoctor: During the past two weeks, have you felt down or depressed nearly every day?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not sure I’d say nearly every day. I’ve had some low moments, of course, but I try not to dwell on them. Maybe I’m just tired or frustrated with myself. I don’t want to overstate it. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you gotten lost in places that should be familiar to you?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve gotten lost exactly… not in places I know. It’s more that my head feels foggy, and I mix up simple things—appointment times, dates for the exhibit, or whether I already called a volunteer. That’s really what worried me enough to come in, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually tired or low on energy lately?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose I have been more tired than usual. I don’t want to make too much of it, but I wake up tired even if I’ve slept, and by the time I’ve washed up and made a little breakfast, I feel like I need to sit down again. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any numbness or tingling in your hands or feet?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, my hands fall asleep if I’ve leaned on them funny, but nothing unusual that I’ve noticed. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any recent blood tests showing a thyroid problem or low vitamin B12?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, doctor. I don’t think anyone has told me I had a thyroid problem or low B12 recently… at least not that I remember. I may have had routine bloodwork, but I don’t want to say the wrong thing. Would that be something you could check in my records?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
